# V1 Handoff Findings Ledger

Open defects, gaps and blockers found while landing the all-WIP consolidation
(PR 116) for the 2026-10-05 move to a new development machine. Each finding
records its observed evidence and explicit acceptance obligations. Order work by
the release dependencies below and register current ownership through
`docs/ops/MULTI_AGENT_COORDINATION.md`; finding numbers are stable identities,
not execution priority. When a repair lands, retain its stable ID, dated evidence and exact qualification scope in the
fixed history, with remaining family obligations linked to their existing open
owners. Do not delete findings or discard earlier failure or measurement evidence
when status changes.

Evidence paths under `C:\Molt\...` exist only on the Windows host that
produced them; the public CI run IDs are enough to reproduce each finding.

From 2026-10-06 the primary development host is macOS (Apple Silicon). The
Windows host stays available for platform testing. Host paths are in
`docs/agent/ORCHESTRATION.md` (Canonical paths).

## Release decision and exit scope

**V1.0 release is held.** This ledger is the authoritative open-findings and
exit-status index. Passing local checks or implementing a producer does not
qualify a release. The machine-checked requirements remain owned by
[the public contract](../spec/PUBLIC_CONTRACT_V1.md),
[phase exit requirements](../../config/phase_exit_requirements.toml), and
[the release acceptance matrix](../../config/release_acceptance_matrix.toml).
Every outstanding correctness, security, resource, performance, packaging and
portability finding below is part of the V1.0 exit scope. No finding may be
accepted as debt, waived or deferred past release qualification. Close a finding only
after its complete consumer family is integrated and its stated acceptance
evidence passes; retain its stable ID, integrated repair and explicit unverified
coordinates. Consolidation must preserve every requirement and machine-consumed ID.

This closure standard applies retrospectively to every earlier finding,
including the historical fixed rows below. A landed repair does not establish
closure of its bug family. Closure requires a failure-mechanism search through
sibling implementations, producers and consumers; one corrected authority;
independent regression controls that exercise the actual boundary; and explicit
coverage of affected target and platform cells. Recurring integration or proof
failures must be traced to their shared cause as well. Reconcile uncovered
instances and missing qualification into the existing owning open requirement;
retain the historical repair evidence without treating it as release acceptance.

Integration decision (2026-10-10 UTC): current main is the canonical base and
one parent integrator owns reconciliation, qualification and landing. The
qualified ABI/supervisor unit landed at `9d943eddd`, reconciled with `50d52e471` and preserving its
newer Windows invocation/custody, R6 admission, cache-validation and intrinsic
retirement changes. Other committed branches and private runtime/measurement
capsules retain their source custody; their existence is not release acceptance.

Historical CI `38050591959` at `ed9c7c308` remains red: the Rust micro partition has
777 passes, one import-error allocation-denial fixture failure and two ignores;
Windows reports changed CPython runtime/import selection in its installed-record
hash test; macOS reports two raw-child-stream timeout failures; WASM reports a
10.39 MB shared runtime against the unchanged 5 MB limit and finally/pending
observer parity failure. These remain assigned to their existing release
obligations. The current integration does not claim to close those failures.

The successor landed at `df5226c5f` and repairs the import/sequence/dictionary allocation-denial fixtures
through the real emergency C-API channel. All three changed tests pass in
independent Linux/aarch64 dev-fast micro-runtime processes, as does the sparse
dictionary snapshot ownership control. Native C build custody is now declared
for nineteen additional confirmed native-runtime command/lane consumers;
direct and wrapped admission retain the declarations. WASM-only and frontend-only
commands keep their distinct tool requirements. These are development-side
checks and add no emitted-binary work.

The unused pass-crate BOLT placeholder and both exports are retired; the existing
native-link BOLT pipeline remains authoritative and its sixteen focused checks
pass. The removed code had no production caller, fabricated a speedup estimate,
and emitted a comment-only macOS order file. Measured macOS profile-derived
ordering remains open. The ABI lane now collects every test executable's result
with `--no-fail-fast`; its topology and admission cohort passes 211 checks.
Bootstrap-parser and Windows launch-spelling fixtures retain their independent
identity/output oracles; their focused cohorts pass 38 and 50 checks on macOS.
Actual Windows replay remains required.

The Linux/aarch64 Rust replay at `b52360cf0` has complete native custody and closed
descendants: WASM backend, pass, and ownership-contract cohorts pass 305, 946,
and 286 tests respectively. The full partition still fails because
`object::accessors::tests::frame_storage_collects_inline_and_dictionary_cycles_before_scope_exit`
segfaults. A fresh-process exact replay reproduces that crash without an RSS
violation. Exact-image fault tracing locates the crash before collection: native
pointer decoding sign-extends a valid Linux/AArch64 stack address whose bit 47
is set. The runtime's decoder and six native-codegen sites share this defect.
The unsigned 48-bit address correction, all-profile wide-address rejection,
complete native consumer migration and incompatible-artifact ABI witness update
pass the Linux/aarch64 runtime partition at `d43dab3cb`: 781 runtime tests
(two existing ignores), 305 WASM backend, 946 pass, 286 ownership-contract and
one raw-symbol check. This includes the original crashing frame-cycle case.
The failing image and fault evidence remain retained. The final checked boxer
is explicitly inlined at `b26875812`; alternating optimized macOS/AArch64
microprobes measure decode medians of 0.365/0.366 ns and boxing medians of
0.391/0.403 ns before/after. The decoder remains one machine instruction and
boxing has no added hot-path call. These advisory samples establish neither
an end-to-end speedup nor cross-platform performance acceptance.

The compiler partition at `493082f25` passes all 1,386 tests with complete
native custody, no violations and closed descendants. It exercises the actual
Cranelift result, object-model address boundaries and incompatible ABI link
witnesses in both threading modes. Six prior AArch64 execution failures shared

as a raw linker argument. The shared native/LLVM helper now uses an actual

Linux/aarch64 with LLVM 22.1.8, including both shared native-execution fixtures
and the unsigned-address decoder; native custody is complete with no violations
and closed descendants. The Windows supervisor now preserves `NTSTATUS`
through its debug-event continuation boundary, reusing the preserved repair;
its MSVC all-targets cross-check passes with unchanged source and tool images.
Actual macOS/Windows execution remains unverified; these results do not qualify
the full release matrix.

The same compiler replay exposed undeclared Criterion/alloca C compilation.
The existing compiler partition now owns that target-C obligation and the

The final focused cohort passes 113 checks (12 platform skips), with 794 passes

passes on Linux. After the test fixture supplies the real compiler's complete
launcher/content identity, all four Rust/C/C++/combined capture cells pass at
`047cb630f`. The actual GCC C++ compile produces an ELF object under the native
supervisor; removing its `cc1plus` image is independently refused and produces

The full-runtime/MLIR build consumers, cold/warm reuse, and actual macOS/Windows
execution remain release-acceptance obligations under V1-12. These are
strictly development-side checks and add no work to emitted binaries.
Main CI `38063172584` at `df5226c5f` retains the runtime lifecycle cascade:

The preserved GC, thread-identity and joined-service proposals remain unqualified.
The same CI reports a macOS SIGTERM cleanup failure (2,100 other Python tests

operation family must be corrected without weakening host proc-macro linking.

and service migration, compiled execution and cost qualification remain required

The same defect exists in the sequence-snapshot denial fixture. Their successor
checks the real C API for MemoryError identity and negative type matching while
allocation remains denied, preserves pending state until clear, and retains
the successful recovery and reference-ownership oracles. The independent micro
runtime replays above qualify these fixture corrections; they do not qualify
the frame-cycle crash or GC/lifecycle failures.

The Rust job finishes with 481 default-runtime failures; retained stderr separates
437 distinct bootstrap refusals, 22 lifecycle-entry refusals and 22 other failed

to assignment of Python None. Working source preserves the original optional

The runtime-restart native-owner failure occurs while retiring the previous runtime

Adversarial source review also confirms a delayed native-thread startup gap: the
parent execution lease can end before a spawned worker protects its runtime.
Shared workers borrowing an isolate require origin-state lifetime custody too.
The complete spawn/admission/retirement family remains held for repair and
independent concurrency controls before integration. Review also identifies
callback-created thread-local edges during shared-worker cleanup and detached
process pipe workers that can cross runtime retirement. Both belong to this
same ownership repair; a process reservation alone does not close them.

Development-side retained-file consumers now share a finite captured-extent

architectures, all seven native and six WASM products, contamination controls,

publishes truncation warnings after writes, and preserves CPython 3.12 versus

extended differential case executes successfully under CPython 3.12.15; Molt

receipt consumer tests pass, with one skip. All 26 reproducible generators are
current. Three previously unowned header/layout/attestation test files now run
in existing CI jobs. Actual Rust compilation, lifecycle and C-ABI execution,
WASM target execution and performance qualification remain required. The earlier
59-case shared-storage measurement is not a compatible L7 numeric baseline;
current cost collection must remain evidence-only until a valid comparison is
available. The existing L7 and list cost probes remain the measurement owners.

Every change must distinguish development/compiler work from execution in the
shipped end-user binary. Source custody, proof instrumentation and release
evidence belong on the development side. Additional runtime work must implement
a concrete runtime requirement and justify its measured cost; do not charge
development validation to guest startup, allocations or execution.
Eager work requires a concrete correctness requirement or reproducible evidence
that it improves the relevant workload. Otherwise defer it to its first real
consumer, preserve failure atomicity, and avoid duplicate ownership or caches.

CPython source at the declared reference version is the semantic source of
truth. Source-grounded implementation and version-matched independent execution
must agree before a Python semantic finding can close; a fixture or local test
result alone cannot redefine the contract.

The matched independent V1 review also exposed the following distinct families.
These are source-confirmed mechanisms with execution hypotheses stated explicitly;
none is accepted as release debt or as a reason to weaken the declared matrix.

| ID | Finding | Acceptance obligation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| V1-29 | `itertools.tee` attributes shared Python edges to the first owner, clears its iterator/buffer when that owner detaches, and only afterward removes that owner. The buffer is append-only. Its index follows a pointer at a pointer-sized offset, creating an unaligned i64 projection on wasm32. The existing independence case keeps both tees alive. | Reuse the runtime ownership/layout authorities under V1-05/V1-19: shared edges must survive any individual reader, collection must see complete reachability, buffered retention must follow reader lag, and physical projections must have one aligned extent authority. Prove first-reader deletion, peek, mixed reachability, reentry/failure, item retirement and wasm32 execution against the declared CPython references. Measure per-next latency, lockstep/lag memory and allocation density. The source mechanisms are confirmed; the predicted execution/GC consequences still require actual reproduction. |
| V1-30 | Streaming compression creates fresh decoders for chunks, retains output as later input, and does not bound materialization by the requested output length. Compression handles are untyped raw pointer projections distinct from the existing typed native-handle owner. `wbits`, dictionaries, buffer inputs and LZMA memory limits have incomplete consumers; source-only export checks do not qualify these semantics. | Reconcile zlib/LZMA and dependent gzip/bz2/codec, intrinsic, ABI, resource and lifecycle consumers under existing V1-19/V1-18 owners. Use maintained streaming engines and typed ownership; preserve requested formats, bounded memory, errors, EOF/tails and cleanup. Run pinned CPython source tests and independent multichunk, length-limit, wrong-handle, buffer and allocation-denial controls across native/WASM. Measure throughput, allocations and peak live memory. Do not infer type-confusion reachability or target execution from a source read alone. |
| V1-31 | Release requirements enumerate five aggregate phase obligations while the human V1 contract requires every open finding to close. The ledger check enforces identifier uniqueness but does not join open finding state to authenticated closure evidence. | Make the existing release/phase authority consume one canonical finding-status projection and reject any open or unevidenced obligation at final publication. Retain stable historical IDs, explicit cell scope and independent negative controls; avoid a second ledger or hand-maintained obligation registry. Reconcile this join with the positive evidence migration under V1-01/V1-07/V1-25. |

| HF-79 | No builtin call inside a function is specialized. Since 6cf681a1c (2026-09-17), `python_binding_flow.py` creates every function and lambda scope with `activation_namespace_stable=False` and an entry state that invalidates all members, so each global or builtin name read in a function is an activation lookup of unknown identity. The frontend then lowers `len(xs)`, `isinstance(x, int)`, `ord(s[i])` and `print(...)` to `module_get_global` plus `call_func`, and `for i in range(n)` to a generic `iter`/`iter_next` loop instead of a counted loop. The refusal is sound, because any importer can rebind `mod.len` or `builtins.len` before the function runs, but nothing recovers the fast path. The same conservatism applies at module scope after any import or user call: `from helpers import imported` followed later by `alias = alpha` leaves `alias` without a provable callable identity, even when `def alpha` directly precedes it. `molt_module_get_global` (`runtime/molt-runtime/src/builtins/modules.rs`) also allocates an owned `String` for the name on every call, even with tracing off. | IR probes on 2026-10-09 give the same result on origin/main 49d857d61 and the integrator stack. No benchmark record exists after 2026-09-17 (`bench/results`), so the slowdown is unmeasured. | (1) Measure the `bench/` corpus natively against a build that treats function scopes as stable, to size the loss. (2) Make the runtime lookup non-allocating on the hit path. (3) Re-admit specialization behind a runtime guard, as CPython 3.12's `LOAD_GLOBAL_BUILTIN` does: give module globals and the builtins namespace a binding version (or per-name shadow bits) that every insert, delete and rebind updates; in a function whose module source never binds the name, emit one guard and the specialized op, with the dynamic call as the guarded fallback. This touches the binding authority, TIR, every backend and the runtime, so it lands as one change with native and WASM differential proof. |

A call-name prefilter for the encoding gate cannot be exact (`text=True` may be split by comments) and would skip few files. | Wall-clock and cProfile, 2026-10-06, 2026-10-08 and 2026-10-09. | Walk each Python tree once and dispatch to the probes that need it. 2026-10-09: 12.5 s → 9.2 s on this Mac with identical findings (the raise collector now walks only statement blocks, since `raise` cannot occur in an expression; each Rust file's comment and literal spans are scanned once, by the parallel prewarm, instead of once per projection). The stub probe walks each tree once for its `raise` statements and skips the walk when the text has no `raise`; the debt-marker probe tokenizes only the 398 of 1,877 Python files whose text matches a marker. A shared parse cache made the run slower (the stub probe went from 2.2 s to 3.3 s): keeping every source tree alive for the pass costs more in collector scans than the second parse it saves, and `gc.freeze()` after the prewarm gained nothing. |

## Fixed after the handoff

| ID | Defect | Integrated fix and verification boundary |
|----|--------|-----------------------------------------|
| HF-F57 (was HF-33) | Generators had no common command line: most wrote when run bare, three needed `--write`, one `--fix`, each re-implemented check/write, and CI paid one interpreter and one proof command per generator (26). All 29 now implement one contract (`generated_outputs() -> {path: text}`, entered through `generator_main`, which requires exactly one of `--check`/`--write`; a bare run exits 2). `tools/generators.py check|write` renders them in one interpreter in `upstream_generators` order, the proof plan last, under one memory-guard sentinel (110 s -> 57 s on an idle machine), and is the single CI command (`repository.generators`, 26 generators). The meta-gate rejects a generator without the contract and any per-generator CI re-check; `generate_command` is deleted and every documented bare invocation now says `--write`. Files a generator stops rendering fail closed instead of being deleted. Remaining cost is rendering itself: `gen_cpython_coverage` 8 s, `gen_stringprep_tables` 7.6 s, `gen_unicode_width` 7.2 s. | `tools/generator_io.py`, `tools/generators.py`; `check_gating` in `tools/check_generator_manifest.py`. Integrated at a6480a190 and documented at 820bbeccd. The 29 declared/26 CI generator counts and 110 s to 57 s total, with 8 s/7.6 s/7.2 s rendering components, are dated upstream measurements, not a new benchmark or release performance result. Reconciliation must reuse this single authority; rendering allocation, retained publication security and affected platform cells remain under V1-07/V1-25. |
| HF-F58 | On Windows, `gen_proof_plan.py --write --from-index` always failed and left a stale `.git/index.lock` that blocked every later Git command: `fenced_git_index` unlinked the lock while its own descriptor was open, which Windows refuses (WinError 32). The two fence tests in `tests/tools/test_release_supply_chain.py` failed on Windows for the same reason. The lock is now opened delete-on-close (`O_TEMPORARY`) on Windows, so closing it, or the process dying, removes it; POSIX keeps the identity-checked unlink. The trailing-space index-path test is skipped on Windows, where Win32 strips trailing spaces from names. | `fenced_git_index` in `tools/release/git_source_snapshot.py`. Integrated at 702b65c99. This preserves the historical Windows diagnosis, source correction and justified trailing-space fixture exception. No new parent-run Windows execution, abrupt process-death or lock-substitution qualification is claimed; those cells remain under V1-12 and retained publication acceptance. |
| HF-F59 (was HF-70) | The drift gate still read retired exception helpers and omitted the replacement schema from its trigger inputs. | The gate now projects the canonical object-model schema, including target-platform aliases, and rejects alias chains and ordinal matches that do not return the declared table. The schema, shared Rust scanner and both C-API headers trigger the existing rule. The obsolete root-probe subprocess exemption is deleted from the shared policy. All five drift categories, 31 focused tests, 12 source-input dispatch controls and staged generated freshness pass. This correction is development-only; broader process-custody and release-platform qualification remain open under V1-12. Landed on main at c2079e29d. The final complete unit additionally exercised both CLI output modes and the 21 affected shared subprocess-policy consumers, for 52 tests total; it preserves the target namespace superset and rejects shadowed ordinal matches. Independent review closes R1-R4, including the obsolete subprocess exemption migration. These development-only checks do not qualify broader V1-12 or release-platform cells. |
| HF-67 | Strict IR admission outgrew the Rust rejection-applicability audit, which reported eight new violations. | Integrated at dbbdc92d9: the audit consumes registered validator and operand projections with independent wire expectations and adversarial mutations; the baseline was not relaxed. This is source-verified historical repair evidence. V1-25/V1-26 retain runtime, CI and semantic-family qualification. |
| HF-03 | Unknown operations silently became Copy, and a later gate rejected legitimate Luau-private operations. | The strict admission authority is integrated at 7509d1325: unknown operations fail at frontend, middle-end and wire/backend boundaries; the declared Luau-private family remains admitted only at its internal function/SSA boundary, and canonical registration rejects unknown kinds before and after frontend optimization and at every checked transport/backend boundary. The singleton follow-up from PR118 is integrated and tracked under V1-26. Candidate7350f43ead and its hosted merge share treeecfbf8dd0; the strict singleton control, 94 backend-bin cases and 194 Luau library cases pass. The default-truth partition ignored the executable Luau control, but the later independent compiler-authorities partition explicitly included ignored tests and executed it successfully in hosted run37661157711. The integrated local dependency closure matches that successful partition; the earlier unexecuted classification was incorrect. Runtime failures, abnormal termination and failed platform/infrastructure receipts remain mandatory release work under their owning findings; bounded library successes do not qualify them. Historical main qualification recorded at c2079e29d also passed 2,633 Darwin arm64/Rust1.99/LLVM22 affected backend/IR library tests after the exact three pinned-Lune controls with 11 explicit ignores, 38 backend integration cases including five-backend rejection and native ABI/link witnesses, and 733 frontend/generator cases; the unchanged global StringPrep generator check separately failed after the host interpreter update and remains open under V1-22. Operation projections and actual staged proof-plan freshness passed in that earlier generation; independent Astra review found no incomplete consumer migration in that bounded strict-admission unit. The gate runs on the compiler side, and unexecuted target/release cells and execution-byte custody remain explicit acceptance work. These bounded receipts remain valid for their unchanged inputs; failed hosted/runtime cells retain their own release obligations. |
| HF-25 | Partial commits used working-tree authority instead of the actual staged producer and source. Review also found indirect output overwrite, private Git hook execution, whitespace-truncated index selection and valid blob data accepted under the wrong object ID. | `74d2194b5`, verified on `origin/main`, uses immutable staged Git snapshots, source-only imports, exact output custody, verified object IDs and one recoverable two-output publication under Git's selected index lock. The actual staged freshness check passed; focused real-Git/publication checks passed (11), as did release-family checks (215 and 280, with 6 capability skips). Independent adversarial review covered hooks, poisoned imports, source/output mutation, lock replacement, rollback and corrupt SHA-1/SHA-256 objects. All 22 release scratch scopes now use the existing physical-directory custody authority. The current-main replay also proves the complete staged Python capture authority after an isolated source-closure failure exposed an omitted tool-release consumer. Release/custody consumer checks passed 537 with six capability skips; generator and registry consumers passed 734, and all nine actual staged-source/publication controls passed after repair. This development-only lock fences cooperative Git writers; it does not establish kernel execution or byte-use identity. The selected index is revalidated under its exclusive lock. The recorded complete generator fanout required the pinned Acorn bootstrap; that prerequisite remains part of the historical receipt scope. Full release acceptance remains open, and this development-only mechanism adds no emitted-binary work. |
| HF-F61 | The Python lock upgrade moved `uv.lock` to revision 5 (uv 0.12), and the lock identity reader accepted only revisions 1-3, so Platform Portability failed with "uv.lock schema is unsupported". | `SUPPORTED_UV_LOCK_REVISIONS` in `src/molt/python_uv_lock_identity.py` names the set for the reader and the payload validator; a test binds the repository lock's revision to it and proves an unknown revision fails closed. de67a2b93 |
| HF-F62 | 544f07439 admitted the runtime-neutral `missing` singleton for every target, but the Rust emitter had no arm for it, so admitted IR reached the unsupported-op catch-all and the HF-67 structural ratchet regressed (applicability 0→5, lowering gaps 0→1). CI missed it for a day because Docs Gates failed earlier in each run. | The Rust backend lowers `missing` to a `MoltValue::Missing` singleton (truthy, equal only to itself, like the native plain-`object` sentinel); an executed test proves the lowering and fails on the catch-all without it. The ratchet passes at its unchanged zero baseline. |
| HF-F63 | A repo sentinel joined its scan thread for at most max(0.5 s, 2×poll) on exit, so a slow process-table scan could outlive the guard and then trip it or kill a group after its owner had read the verdict. On the macOS CI runner this put a pgid from an earlier test into a later test's recorder (`test_repo_process_sentinel_rejects_codex_parented_sibling_group`). | Exit now revokes trip and kill authority under a lock before the bounded join, and a scan acts on violations only while it holds that lock with the guard active. A deterministic test stalls a scan across exit and proves it neither trips nor kills; it fails without the lock. A fixture that hand-built the sentinel with `object.__new__` now uses the real constructor. |
| HF-F64 | The perf scoreboard pinned its Cargo target to `<checkout>/target/sessions/perfscore`; under hosted custody dx rejects any checkout-owned target dir, so 43 `test_perf_profile_selection.py` cases failed once Docs Gates reached them. | `_perfscore_build_env` drops ambient and checkout targets and lets the canonical bench env place the pinned session under the custody-selected artifact root; a test proves the delegation and fails on the old code. |
| HF-F65 (was HF-54) | The MLIR crate did not build on macOS: `melior-macro` failed with `ld: library 'zstd' not found`, because the managed LLVM prefix had been built against Homebrew zstd. | `tools/bootstrap_llvm.py` already configures `LLVM_ENABLE_ZSTD=OFF`; the managed 22.1.8 prefix on this Mac was rebuilt (684 s) and now names only `-lm -lz -lxml2`. After clearing the stale `mlir-sys`/`tblgen` build-script caches (see HF-64), `mlir.test.backend` passes 42 tests on macOS, 2026-10-08. A host with an older zstd-linked prefix must move it aside and rerun the bootstrap. |
| HF-F66 | `lzma-sys` linked the host's liblzma whenever pkg-config found one, so runtime builds depended on the build machine; on a Mac with Homebrew xz, runtime publication refused `-llzma` as ambiguous (`liblzma.5.dylib` beside `liblzma.a`) and `molt build` failed. | Both `xz2` dependencies enable its `static` feature, so liblzma always builds from the bundled source; the other `-sys` crates were already bundled or pure Rust. The `binascii.crc32` differential run builds and passes on that Mac, 2026-10-08. |
| HF-F67 | The serial pytest session's repo sentinel shares the module functions that unit tests patch, so its thread read one test's fake process groups and terminated them during the next test (`assert [987654] == []` on macOS, run 37826716896, after HF-F63's exit fix). | `RepoProcessMemorySentinel.paused()` withholds scanning and, under the authority lock, the action of a scan in flight; the ten test modules that fake process data use the `session_sentinel_paused` fixture. A deterministic test stalls a scan, pauses, and proves nothing acts until the pause ends; it fails without the check. |
| HF-F68 (was HF-31) | `claim_lane.py` and `claims_status.py` each kept a copy of the claims status vocabulary and a log parser, and both skipped a row with an unknown status. Ten log rows used BLOCKED, DROPPED, CLOSED or STEP2-LANDED, so `--check` on a lane whose newest row was BLOCKED reported the older row's state. | `tools/claims_status.py` owns the vocabulary and the one parser; `parse_rows` raises `ClaimsLogError` for a row outside it, `claim_lane.py` reads through it and refuses a field with `|` or a newline, and `agent_coordination.py context` reports the error as `invalid_status`. The ten rows carry the status their notes support (RELEASED, MEASURED_IMPLEMENTATION_RETIRED, FALSIFIED, SUPERSEDED, PROGRESS), and CLAIMS.md §7 states the closed vocabulary. `tests/tools/test_claims_status.py` parses the real log; the new `repository.agent-coordination` command runs the claims tests in CI. |
| HF-F69 (was HF-69) | Typed frontend fast paths rejected a call at compile time (MOLT-FE002) when its argument count or keywords did not match, for example `s.add()`, `getattr(x)` and `"a".lower(1)`. CPython compiles these calls and raises `TypeError` when they run, so a program can catch it; Molt refused to build it. `len()` alone had moved to the runtime binder in 6cf681a1c, which made two diagnostic tests fail. | All 49 shape checks in the call-dispatch visitors now lower the call through the runtime binder; the dead `list.index` keyword loop and the `field()` positional check are gone; the 16 checks for an operand that cannot lower report OPERAND_VALUE. MOLT-FE002 is retired: `[[retired]]` in `frontend_diagnostics.toml` reserves it, and the generator rejects a gap or a reused code. `tests/test_frontend_diagnostics.py` checks 18 call shapes against CPython and a real rejection (`nonlocal` at module level) on both targets; `tests/differential/basic/call_shape_type_errors.py` covers the runtime result. This records source integration, not merged target execution or closure of ordinary-keyword and argument-evaluation gaps under V1-26. |
| HF-F70 (was HF-74) | The frontend rejected `class A(int, int): pass` at compile time (TYPE_FORM, "Duplicate base class int"), while CPython raises `TypeError: duplicate base class int` when the class statement runs, so a program could not catch it. The check compared base names, so it also missed an aliased base (`class A(Base, Alias)`) and would reject two different classes that share a name. | Repeated bases now build dynamically like any multi-base class, and `molt_class_set_base` (`class_model/hierarchy.rs`) raises the CPython `TypeError` by object identity. `tests/test_frontend_diagnostics.py` checks both forms against CPython; `tests/differential/basic/class_duplicate_base_type_error.py` covers the runtime message. The other TYPE_FORM rejections guard an operand that did not lower, a failed `unparse`, or a type-alias target the grammar cannot produce, so none mirrors a run-time error. |
| HF-F71 (was HF-40) | Molt installed `sccache` on a machine without asking: every DX environment setup downloaded the pinned release into the toolchain root when it was missing. Two helpers resolved `sccache` (`dx._provision_sccache`, which downloaded, and `cargo_execution._pinned_sccache`, which only looked). The WASM tools named in the original finding are already discovery-only (PATH, then the pinned release). | `dx.pinned_sccache` is the one discovery-only helper for both consumers; a missing release builds without the cache and warns once with `python -m molt.tool_releases provision sccache`. CI provisions it explicitly in `setup-project` (not on Windows, where Molt leaves it off). `tests/test_dx_sccache_wrapper.py` proves discovery makes no network request and creates nothing. The proof queue still provisions the tools a plan row declares, because the plan names them explicitly. |
| HF-F72 (was HF-58) | `molt --help` took 0.82 s on this Mac (1.8 s on 2026-10-06): the console script imported the whole `molt.cli` package (293 ms) and only then restarted the interpreter to fix `PYTHONHASHSEED`, so every molt process imported the CLI twice. | The console script is now `molt.cli_entry:main`, a standard-library launcher that owns the hash-seed contract and restarts before the CLI loads; `molt.cli.entrypoint` calls the same `ensure_hash_seed` for `python -m molt.cli`. The old helpers, constants and facade entries in `molt.cli` are deleted. Median `molt --help` is 0.46 s (5 runs), a 44% cut; a probe script shows seed 0 applied with identical string hashes across runs, and `tests/cli/test_cli_smoke.py` proves the launcher does not load `molt.cli`. Importing the CLI itself (293 ms: frontend pipeline, `_wasm_abi_generated` 82 ms) is the next startup cost. The V1-28 reconciliation (2026-10-10) then removed the restart: compiler IR and backend cache payloads do not depend on the host seed, so `src/molt/cli_entry.py` is deleted, the `molt` script is `molt.cli:main` again, and `MOLT_HASH_SEED`/`MOLT_HASH_SEED_APPLIED` are retired and fail closed. Real-CLI cold/warm builds under seeds 0, 1, 42, 12345 and two random seeds give byte-identical IR, msgpack and cache keys (serial and parallel frontend, JSON and msgpack codecs). With `PYTHONHASHSEED` unset, `--help` through a script entry takes 0.79 s median instead of 0.88-0.90 s (8 warm samples, two rounds, this Mac). |
| HF-F73 | The frontend CFG computed dominators as a full set per block, initialized to every block, so memory and time grew quadratically with function size: lowering `src/molt/gpu/tensor.py` (2,667 lines; a 12,461-op module body) peaked at 5.3 GB, all in `build_cfg`, and one test file peaked at 5.4 GB, enough to kill the 7 GB macOS CI runner. | `cfg_analysis` now stores one immediate dominator per reachable block (Cooper, Harvey and Kennedy, 2001) and answers `CFGGraph.dominance.dominates(a, b)` in O(1) from dominator-tree visit numbers; both consumers moved to it. The upstream measurement reports `tensor.py` lowering at 213 MB (25x less) and 3.7 s instead of 4.8 s, with byte-identical IR for it and 120 differential programs; this is historical evidence, not a measurement of the merged lazy CFG/cache implementation. `tests/test_frontend_midend_passes.py` checks `dominates` against the path definition of dominance on 300 random CFGs. |
| HF-F74 (was HF-81) | A file-lock release could return while the lock was still held. When two threads released one handle, the second saw `released` set by the first and returned before the first had unlocked, closed and dropped the in-process mutex, so a caller that then acquired the lock got `None`. `test_terminal_publication_pins_custody_against_postcheck_transfer` failed this way once on hosted Linux. | `_release_file_lock` now records the handle in `_RELEASING_FILE_LOCK_HANDLES` until the unlock completes, and a concurrent release waits on the lifecycle condition until it leaves (copies never wait; the child reset clears the table). `test_duplicate_release_returns_only_after_the_lock_is_free` holds the first release mid-unlock and failed before the fix. The two tests that run the child-side fork reset now run in a fresh interpreter, and the fork-guard test checks its own lock: a memory-guard thread in the pytest process takes the suite lease's adoption lock, so process-wide lock counts are not test-owned. |
| HF-F75 (was HF-38) | `tests/test_memory_guard_tool.py` took over 25 minutes and failed four termination and sampler cases on macOS (2026-10-06). | It now passes 326 cases in 3.5 s on the Mac, and on hosted macOS and Linux in `python.unit.harness` (CI 37897170405, whose macOS harness failures were only the diff guard's host-memory cases fixed in 5cad60e92). |
| HF-F76 (was HF-83) | The intrinsic-first stdlib stubs had no checked authority. Two ungated sync tools only created missing stubs from a pre-hardening template, the 275 committed stubs fell into 13 shapes, and the surface tests pinned contradictory forms. The full `tools/check_stdlib_intrinsics.py` gate was red on main: 68 union modules (65 `idlelib.idle_test` modules and `msilib.schema`, `sequence` and `text`) were missing, and `msilib` was a module where CPython ships a package. A CI rewrite (6c21d2717, 2026-03-25) had dropped that gate, and nothing ran it since. | Stub census and the full gate on a HEAD export, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: `tools/gen_stdlib_stubs.py` owns every stub through one template. A stub requires `molt_capabilities_has`, binds nothing and raises the canonical gap error; a package stub raises `AttributeError` for its union submodules, so `from pkg import sub` can load a lowered child; a platform-only module raises CPython's `ModuleNotFoundError` off its platform. The generator writes a stub for each missing union module and refuses a stub outside the union or of the wrong kind; `tools/generators.py check` gates it, and the manifest declares the family as an `output_family`, so the audit still counts every stub as debt. `tests/test_stdlib_stubs.py` holds the files to the generator byte for byte and loads all 343 stubs in one child interpreter; it replaces 19 hand-picked test files (13 ran in `python.unit.surface-contracts`, six were backlog). `msilib` moved to its package form. CI runs the full gate again as `python.static.stdlib-intrinsics` (`--critical-allowlist`). The new stubs make hidden gaps countable, so three counts rose: intrinsic-partial 838 to 906 (budget 875 to 906), `debt_markers_total` 334 to 403 and `python_stub_surfaces_total` 391 to 460. Scattered 3.12 assumptions fell from 273 files to 213. |
| HF-F77 | Docs Gates failed intermittently with "source tree changed or is dirty" (runs on 2026-10-08 and 37903182470), and the log gave no reason: the executor printed nothing on a global stop, and the receipt named no path. The executor's probe returns the dirty Git status entries, the failure reason names them, and a failed run prints each failing command and its reason. |
| HF-F78 (was HF-90) | 98 child-process surface probes carried the same hand-written fake `_intrinsics` resolver and their own intrinsic table, a further 18 tests built other fakes, and the in-process probes for `_compression`, `_decimal` and `_locale` left their fake in `sys.modules` for later tests on the worker. A module that started requiring an anchor broke every table that loaded it. | `git grep -l '_molt_intrinsics = {' tests`, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: `tests/stdlib_intrinsic_registry.py` installs the real resolver (`src/molt/stdlib/_intrinsics.py`) in strict mode with the anchors the intrinsic manifest declares (every `molt_*_runtime_ready`, `molt_stdlib_probe`, the capability checks), and a probe passes only the behavior it exercises; `intrinsic_registry()` restores what it replaced. The three in-process files asserted stub behavior on modules lowered since and were deleted. Pass and fail sets are unchanged (104 passed); the nine failures left are stale oracles of their own (missing behavior intrinsics such as `molt_glob`, a five-argument `molt_bisect_left`, a relative import), not anchors. The other 18 doubles stay on purpose: they record calls, inject resolver failures or model `runtime_active` states. |
| HF-F79 | Nine HF-73 surface tests failed on stale oracles, and five stdlib modules (`difflib`, `gettext`, `html`, `sys`, `textwrap`) left the `_require_intrinsic` helper bound in their namespace (so compiled programs saw `sys._require_intrinsic`, which CPython lacks); the helper-lane rule also matched only the `globals().pop` spelling and flagged nine modules that release it with `del`. | HF-73 surface run, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: the five modules release the helper; the rule recognizes either release structurally. `bisect` now checks Molt's `_bisect` against CPython's (signatures, `lo`/`hi`/`key` cases, keyword-only `key`) with CPython supplying the five-argument intrinsic ABI; `collections` gains the `molt_dict_count_elements` reference; batch `g` compares `difflib` with CPython's. | HF-F80 (was HF-89) | `verify_source_inventory` refused any permission the Git index lacked, so a checkout made under umask 002 (Ubuntu's default with user-private groups, and the `molt-linux` VM) failed with "Compiler source mode changed: rust-toolchain.toml", and so would an installed Molt: the README installs the platform wheel with pip, which writes files as `0o666 & ~umask`. | `molt-linux` VM, 2026-10-09: 12 `test_installed_compiler` cases and `test_release_native_build` failed there. | Fixed: the rule is Git's for checkouts and installs alike: a file keeps the owner's executable bit as recorded and carries no setuid, setgid or sticky bit; the content digest binds the bytes. Its IR inventory regex-scanned frontend source for `MoltOp(kind="X"` and `op.kind == "X"`, so it missed kinds that dispatch tables lower (`StateSet`, `IsPending`, `TaskWait`), took the prose word `count` from the `VecSum` description as an op, and still listed `ExceptionSetCause`, which no op carries since `raise X from Y` became the runtime call `molt_exception_prepare_raise`. The spec parser also read only the first line of each category, so six module ops on continuation lines were never checked. Its frontend "semantic assertions" pinned source spellings and failed when `GUARD_TAG` began to share a branch with `GUARD_TYPE`. The test file ran in no CI command. | `tests/test_debug_verify.py`, 2026-10-09: 1 of 17 failed. | Fixed: the inventory checks every listed op against `FRONTEND_REGISTERED_KINDS`, generated from `op_kinds.toml`; the alias table holds only the seven names that need one and fails on a stale, unregistered or redundant entry; each dedicated frontend lane is serialized through the real serializer. The spec drops `ExceptionSetCause`, the stale 2026-02-11 coverage snapshot and the dead `allow_missing`/`main` lane are deleted, and the 20 tests run in `python.unit.frontend-lowering` on Linux and macOS. |
| HF-F82 | The Node runner's opt-in split-runtime mode (an app module and a separate runtime module, not one linked module) converted each runtime import by `wit/molt-runtime.wit` before the runtime module's own types. That WIT file was hand-written, never validated (466 of its 512 names use underscores, which WIT syntax forbids) and disagreed with the WASM ABI manifest on 21 imports. Seven of them (`closure_load`, `closure_store`, `object_field_get_ptr`, `object_field_set_ptr`, `object_field_init_ptr`, `guarded_field_init_ptr`, `object_set_class`) declared a u32 pointer where the export takes an i64, so the runner turned the BigInt into a Number and the call threw `TypeError: Cannot convert 4096 to a BigInt`. The docs called the file the canonical ABI. | `tests/test_wasm_runner_table_base.py` WAT fixture, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: each import converts by the type of the export it calls, read from the runtime module's type section, and a direct-export fallback uses its export's type; `run_wasm.js` uses the loader bridge's normalizers, which now know only WebAssembly value types. The WIT file and its parser are deleted, and the docs name `wasm_abi_manifest.toml` as the import authority, with WIT to be generated from it. The 19 runner fixture tests, which no CI command ran, now run as `wasm.host.runner-fixtures`; each fixture publishes its link facts with the backend `wasm.build.backend` builds (a `cargo run` per fixture built a second, dev-profile backend, and on a cold runner four tests timed out queued behind it). |
| HF-F83 | `test_stable_snapshot_rejects_midstream_source_change` replaced `sha256` on the global `hashlib` module with a hasher that takes no data argument, so while it ran the pytest memory guard (which hashes a path on Linux when no state root is configured) raised a `TypeError` and the `python.unit.runtime-artifacts` worker crashed with an INTERNALERROR; a wasi-sdk test patched `hashlib.file_digest` the same way. | `molt-linux` VM, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: both install a private `hashlib` view in the module under test only (`process_wide_test_patches` 269 to 267); the full command passes on Linux (2,177 tests). |
| HF-F84 | The split-app optimize cache key concatenated raw bytes with text markers, so `(app = X + "\0reference\0" + Y, reference = Z)` and `(app = X, reference = Y + "\0reference\0" + Z)` shared one key, as did a missing and an empty reference module; a hit could return an app optimized against another reference. The runtime tree-shake key used the same unbounded encoding, and a miss named none of its inputs. | `tools/wasm_link_optimizer_policy.py`, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: both keys hash canonical JSON over named components, the encoding the final-link key already used (schemas `runtime-tree-shake-v8`, `split-app-optimize-v7`); bytes enter as their SHA-256. | HF-F85 (was part of HF-87) | The dead-code ratchet was red on main and ran in no proof-plan command: it keyed each site by its ordinal in the file, so an unrelated mask above a site renamed it and staled the registry (15 unwaived sites, 14 stale entries). Its regex also counted masks inside comments and string literals. | `python3 tools/dead_code_allow_ratchet.py`, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: a site ID names the item the mask covers (`fn:name`, `field:name`, `impl:<header>`, `inner`), a mask a generator emits inside a string is keyed by the generating function, comments are skipped, and an unnameable shape fails closed. `--update` keeps live waivers, drops stale ones and demands an owner and a waiver for each new site; the 15 new sites got specific waivers (shared test-support sources, and the three target-conditional functions that HF-87 keeps open). The registry holds 170 sites (baseline 173 before); the ratchet runs as `repository.dead-code-ratchet` and its 21 tests in `repository.tooling-tests`. |
| HF-F86 (was HF-86) | `tools/check_stdlib_intrinsics.py` took 24 s on this Mac and 250 s on a hosted Linux runner, and CI runs it on every PR. It analyzed all 949 stdlib modules serially and cached nothing, although the compiler already caches the same per-module facts by source digest and tooling fingerprint. Two cheaper costs went first (27.3 s to 24.4 s): fixed `IntFlag` masks composed per call, and an AST hash for a cache key nobody reused. | cProfile and timing of the full gate, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: the gate reads the compiler's facts cache, so builds and the gate share one analysis. `module_graph_cache.warm_stdlib_intrinsic_source_facts` fills it in parallel (memory-bounded workers, one interleaved batch each, at least 32 modules a worker), and every batch and the final classification run inside one tooling-fingerprint transaction (without it, each module recomputed the fingerprint and the gate took 215 s). | HF-F87 (was HF-92) | `test_tree_shake_runtime_reuses_cached_result` failed intermittently on the hosted Linux runner: the second call missed the cache the first had just published. The miss diagnostics (HF-F84) named the cause on the next run: only the `tool` component differed, the digest of `tools/wasm_link.py`'s import closure. Where a module has an unknown relative-import anchor, that closure inventories the whole local source domain, and the resolver counted every directory as a namespace member, `__pycache__` included. A fresh checkout gains `tools/__pycache__` when Python first writes bytecode, so the digest changed while tests ran; every tooling identity built from the same closure could flip the same way on a fresh install. | PR #140 run 37928372708, and a pristine `git archive` copy, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: the source inventory skips PEP 3147's bytecode cache directory, which holds no sources. Run alone, the file exited 1 at session end for the same reason; the join had checked only its pass count. `tests/e2e/test_falcon_ocr_targets.py` held the same insert. | CI run 37942210451; local reruns, 2026-10-09. | Fixed: both files keep only `src` on the path (`tinygrad` lives at `src/tinygrad`). `tests/conftest.py` used to drop the root silently before each file was collected, which missed an insert made at import or inside a test; it now fails the collection of a module, or the test, that leaves the root behind and names the authorities that load Molt stdlib files by path. | HF-F91 (was HF-75) | The frontend accepted `await` outside an async function and lowered it to `ASYNC_BLOCK_ON` (run the awaitable to completion), an undeclared extension; CPython rejects it at compile time. The placement test, `is_async()`, was also true in sync generators, so `await` and `async for` there compiled as coroutine code, and `async for`/`async with` outside a coroutine raised a Molt `FrontendRejection` instead of CPython's `SyntaxError`. A second flag, `async_context`, inherited into generator expressions, rejected async generator expressions that CPython allows in any scope. | One predicate, `_in_coroutine_body` (the frame plan's kind is `ASYNC` or `ASYNC_GENERATOR`, and no class body is open), decides placement for `await`, `async for`, `async with` and async comprehensions, with CPython's messages, lines and columns; `async_context` and `is_async_context` are deleted. `ASYNC_BLOCK_ON` is gone from `op_kinds.toml`, both generated registries, the serializer, the native backend and the WASM op-loop table; the `molt_block_on` runtime function and its WASM import stay, because the `asyncio` intrinsic uses them. `tests/test_frontend_async_placement.py` compares 23 placements against CPython's `compile()`. | HF-F92 (was HF-100) | The typed-IR verifier reported 16 of 995 differential cases as `non-dominating-definition`, 14 of them false: its definite-definition dataflow treated `raise` as falling through, so a handler arm that re-raises appeared to reach the code after the `try` through an exception check's fall-through edge, which never runs with an exception pending. | The dataflow now tracks definitions per pending-exception mode: `raise` leaves only the pending mode, `exception_clear` and `exception_enter_handler` (the runtime ops that drop a pending exception) leave only the clear mode, every other op may raise, an exception check's fall-through carries the clear mode and its exception edge the pending mode. The effects are declared once in `op_kinds.toml` (`simpleir_pending_exception_raise_kinds`, `simpleir_pending_exception_clear_kinds`) and generated into Rust. New Rust tests cover a re-raising arm and a read between the raise and the check. The full suite gives 986 pass, 2 fail (both true defects, HF-104) and 7 unsupported, with a 136 MiB verifier peak. |
| HF-F93 (was HF-84) | `tools/gen_stdlib_module_union.py --check` could pass on no host. The generator queried live interpreters through `uv run --python`, so its content followed the host build: on this Mac, uv's 3.12.15 omits `msilib`, its free-threaded 3.14.4 (`python3.14t`) omits `idlelib/idle_test`, and 3.13.12 adds `pydoc_data.module_docs`. `ci_checkable = false` hid all three. | The generator derives each supported version from the CPython source pinned in `config/cpython_regrtest_sources.toml`, which keeps 3.12 at v3.12.13 and adds v3.13.16 (`cbc944f4b`) and v3.14.8 (`8e6e75d91`): module names from `Python/stdlib_module_names.h`, packages from the directories under `Lib/`, and `.py` modules and packages from the `Lib/` tree, with the old query's rules. `config/cpython_stdlib_snapshot.json` (103 KB) commits those inputs with receipts (tag, revision, path, git object id, sha256), so `--check` reads only committed files; the row is now `ci_checkable = true` and renders in 0.03 s inside `repository.generators`. `--refresh-sources` is the one network mode: it fetches each tag without blobs through git, requires the tag to resolve to its pin, and writes nothing when content at an unchanged commit differs from its receipt (6.5 s for three tags). Against the committed files, 3.12 and 3.13 gain `idlelib.idle_test` and its 65 modules, and 3.13 gains `pydoc_data.module_docs`; 3.14 and every cross-version union are unchanged, so the stubs and the stdlib intrinsic gates do not move. The 30 generator tests hold a hand-written oracle tree, a `find_spec` differential on the host stdlib, an offline `--check` with sockets and processes blocked, receipt, pin and refresh fail-closed cases, and a real-git fetch from a local remote; three mutations (the package rule, the offline receipt check, the refresh receipt check) each fail a test. | HF-F142 (was HF-137) | The Python child broker resolved a Windows launch through the child's PATH, PATHEXT and cwd. CPython's `Popen` passes `executable` as lpApplicationName and the `list2cmdline` string as lpCommandLine (`Lib/subprocess.py`, 3.12), and `CreateProcessW` resolves the image in the calling process: its image directory, its current directory (unless `NeedCurrentDirectoryForExePathW` says no, which applies only to a name without a backslash), the system, 16-bit system and Windows directories, then the caller's PATH, appending only `.exe`. The child's `env` and `cwd` take no part, and a given `executable` is completed from the caller's current directory and never searched. So the broker could admit a child-PATH or child-cwd decoy, or a `.com` that PATHEXT ranks first, while Windows ran another image, and it refused launches Windows would run. The hook also split a Windows command line with `shlex`, not CreateProcess's rule. Microsoft's text says `.exe` is not appended to a name with a path, but its own example appends it; on hosted Windows Server 2022 it is appended to both `C:\...\tool` and `./tool`, so HF-137's `./cargo` runs `cargo.exe`. The Node hook shared the child-PATH rule; it launches the broker's selection, but libuv appends `.com` or `.exe` to a selected path without an extension and runs that file. The native supervisor hashes every created image (`CREATE_PROCESS_DEBUG_EVENT`), so a wrong admission was a hook error, not a closure escape under native supervision. | `tools/proof_queue_pkg/windows_createprocess.py` owns the CreateProcessW model; the broker picks the model from the hook's authenticated runtime and the host. On Windows the Python hook reports `executable`, the command line and the caller's facts: image (`GetModuleFileName`), current directory, PATH from the process environment block (`os.putenv` bypasses `os.environ`) and `NeedCurrentDirectoryForExePath`. Where the documentation conflicts or is silent (a path-qualified name without an extension, a relative name with a separator, empty, quoted or relative PATH entries, a directory hit), the broker admits only an image that every reading runs; device, drive-relative, stream, wildcard, traversal and trailing-dot names fail closed. POSIX keeps the child-PATH rule. A Node selection on Windows must have an extension. A table from the documentation checks the model on every host (37 cases). `test_python_hook_broker_admits_the_image_createprocess_runs` sends 15 launches through the same `Popen` call twice, plain and under the real hook and broker; each child is created suspended, and its kernel image (`QueryFullProcessImageNameW`) is the oracle; no child-PATH, child-cwd, PATHEXT or image-directory decoy is admitted. `test_node_selection_names_the_image_libuv_runs` shows libuv running the `.exe` beside an extensionless path. Both pass on hosted Windows in CI 38031471885 attempts 2 and 3 (jobs 114171437224 and 114174710194), with all 58 Windows rows of the custody file; the 65 POSIX rows pass on hosted Linux and macOS (attempt 1). The cell's other failures are not this change: four `test_dx_run_context.py` rows (red on the base run 38027215773), the HF-76 identity-probe census rows, two real-Cargo rows of `tests/test_cargo_quarantine_custody.py` (cold cache, HF-139) and one real queue execution ending rc 2, which moved from `test_proof_queue_exec_records_passed_run` (attempt 2) to `test_guarded_receipt_uses_row_repo_root_and_exact_outer_binary_identity` (attempt 3), the HF-76 receipt symptom. Open: HF-138 (launch surfaces the hook does not judge). |
| HF-F133 (was part of HF-48) | Every guarded command paid a whole-host process census. One `git --version` through `molt_dev_common._run_fast_captured_command` cost 397 ms at 1,980 host processes and 161 ms at 930, against 10 ms raw; with `sampling_scope="owned_tree"` it cost 224 ms and 109 ms. The guard took about seven full samples per command (a stale preflight that could never signal, the repo-sentinel baseline and its scan, the first tree sample, tracked-orphan cleanup, repo-scoped cleanup, the closure proof), and each sample read every process's kernel rows and argv. The adaptive budget also spawned `vm_stat` about three times per command. `tests/test_molt_dev.py` fixtures spent 5-7 s each on seven `git` calls, and `repository.docs-tests` reached 552-600 s on CI. | macOS reads every `kern.proc` row in one `KERN_PROC_ALL` sysctl. A native `ProcessSample` binds argv on first read, checked against the sampled birth (macOS and Linux), and `ProtectedProcessGroups` decides only the groups a caller asks about, so a decision reads argv for its tree and lineage only. The inert preflight and its `MOLT_*_STALE_*` knobs are deleted; a sentinel that does not drain takes no baseline; one post-exit snapshot serves both orphan cleanups and the closure proof until the guard signals; `host_statistics64` replaces `vm_stat`. After, measured back to back on this Mac: 31 ms global and 28 ms owned_tree at 930 processes, 40 ms and 36 ms at 1,980 (guard overhead 386 ms to 30 ms). The sentinel's exit join takes about 0.1 ms per short command: its thread takes no scan before the first poll interval, and a scan costs about 4 ms. `tests/test_molt_dev.py` at `-n 4`: 37.8 s to 12.9 s. The `repository.docs-tests` argv at `-n 4`: 132.1 s to 73.8 s, with the same 14 host-specific failures before and after. New tests: an escaped new-session grandchild is still terminated and tree RSS across it is still enforced (each fails when the post-exit snapshot or the RSS read is broken), argv reads stay inside the tree, and no preflight census runs. Two sentinel fixtures that tied births to the test pid, and three tests that faked the wrong custody module, are fixed. Guard suites (26 files): 1,408 passed on macOS, with one host-layout failure in `test_artifact_cleanup.py` that base shares; 1,405 passed on `molt-linux`, none failed. The `repository.docs-tests` budget is still 600 s on main; PR 150 raises it to 900 s, and it can come back down once both land. |

The runtime Cargo plan adds the toolchain library path the way rustup does. | `rust_toolchain_library_environment` in `src/molt/rust_toolchain.py`. |

| HF-F56 | Pins moved to their newest releases with verified digests: uv 0.12.23, Node 26.10.0, wasm-tools 1.261.0, Binaryen 133, WASI SDK 34 (LLVM 23.1.0, with the vendored wasm builtins and `provenance.toml`). `tools/pin_freshness.py --update` moves each; tests read pins from their authorities instead of restating them. | `tools/pin_freshness.py`; `vendor/wasm-builtins/provenance.toml`. |
| HF-F105 (was HF-51) | `tests/tools/test_toolchain_capture.py::test_toolchain_capture_compact_receipt_allocation_benchmark` compared two allocation peaks that sat 0.3-2.5% apart, and on this Mac the compact path's was the larger: `custody_cas.put_json` held the encoded bytes, then `verify_ref` decompressed the blob it had just written, parsed the whole JSON graph and re-serialized it, so publishing a 4.3 MB capture peaked at about 6.4 times its size. | `put_json` now verifies the published blob by a streamed size and SHA-256 check: a blob is named by the digest of its compressed bytes and gzip is deterministic here, so equal digest proves equal content, including when a concurrent writer won publication. The compact path's peak fell from 0.89 to 0.35-0.38 of the legacy peak (24.1 MB to 9.5 MB on this Mac, 2026-10-09), and the test now asserts less than half, which fails on the old publication. |

Independent review covers all nine production calls and the GNU/musl/BSD declarations. Darwin arm64 qualification at `7509d1325` passes 25 platform tests, including actual-field and signed/unsigned overflow controls, and the runtime/Wasmtime all-target consumer compile closes successfully. The helper, all four caller files and pinned libc inputs are unchanged after rebasing; independent review accepts this bounded conversion invariant. A newer whole-Cargo operation refused unavailable descendant RSS and remains unproven. That native check compiles eight calls; Linux-only AF_ALG is the ninth, statically reviewed but uncompiled here. Linux GNU/musl, other BSD and target execution remain unverified. No allocation or runtime platform dispatch is introduced. Actual graph/bootstrap/harness/generator consumers pass 70 checks; the exact generator fanout renewal passes after the pinned Acorn bootstrap. The dependency-free SDK recompiles and its three configuration tests pass on Darwin arm64/Rust 1.99.0. Independent review accepts this bounded retirement. SDK CLI behavior (V1-24), C API execution and unverified target/release cells remain open. No guest code, allocation or fallback is added; build-time or binary-size improvement is unmeasured. |

The roadmap records the actual two native and two runtime inputs to candidate assembly. ## Preservation

Everything removed while consolidating onto `main` is recoverable from bundles on the Windows host only: `C:\Molt\drift-bundles\handoff-20261005-final.bundle` (all 88 refs before branch deletion) and `handoff-20261005-final-worktrees.bundle` (final snapshots of every removed worktree), plus `stash@{0}` in `C:\Molt\molt-src` (the shared checkout's Sep 30 - Oct 1 working state, superseded by `main`).

Codex's committed work landed through the takeover of `codex/queued-cargo-selection` (PR 119). Its uncommitted work stays in place as donor material, idle since 2026-10-06 to 2026-10-08 and partly superseded by that branch. Reconcile a worktree's files when the owning row's work starts; never apply one wholesale. Triage against the takeover tip on 2026-10-09 counted, per worktree under `~/.codex/worktrees/<name>/molt-src`, the changed files whose patch is already contained, applies cleanly, or conflicts:

| Worktree | Area | Contained | Applies cleanly | Conflicts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `shared-hash-storage` | runtime dict/set storage (V1-27) | 122 | 13 | 51 |
| `ci-source-audit` | runtime and C-ABI fixtures | 22 | 14 | 19 |
| `v1-ffi-retirement` | proof supervisor, queue and guard tooling | 6 | 99 | 53 |
| `v1-resource-contract` | resource contract, CLI and CI wiring | 15 | 48 | 71 |
| `v1-main-landing` | guard and queue tooling, Windows process API | 19 | 41 | 17 |
| `hf03-upstream-reconciliation` | WASI SDK and CLI consumers (17 commits on `codex/v1-coherent-wasi-sdk` beyond the takeover, plus WIP) | 0 | 18 | 9 |

## Release dependency and landing order

Land complete changed authorities as soon as their actual consumers pass. Keep
one integration candidate, reconcile retained donor work against its current
base, and delete each replaced internal path in that same migration. A broader
open release finding does not require holding an independently complete repair;
an unmigrated consumer does. Reuse unchanged accepted inputs rather than
restarting unrelated proofs after every edit.

| Order | Owning work and release consequence | Acceptance boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Finish V1-28 SDK target context and exact Cargo artifact-kind capture, preserving the hash-seed and warning repairs that PR 158 carries. | Actual runtime pair, supported direct Cargo, combined/split source extensions, real native-C compilation, and installed bundle/setup/compiled-runtime consumers. Synthetic selection alone is insufficient. |
| 2 | Integrate the reconciled SOURCE/cache/publication family under V1-01/V1-09/V1-25. Preserve current SDK and final-link cache authorities. | Real receipt/cache refusal when source-derived ABI obligations change with identical runtime bytes, successful commit boundaries, output/source alias controls, migrated fixtures, independent replay and matched compiler-side cost. |
| 3 | Complete workspace, process, source and writer custody through existing V1-01/V1-12/V1-25 authorities; land disjoint complete units. | Wrong-workspace counterexample, installed process lifecycle, sampler failure, native journal preallocation/failure atomicity, retained source/bytecode identity, and actual hardlink/ancestor/publication consumers. |
| 4 | Reconcile runtime resource ownership and physical C publication before dependent numeric/container, sort, codec/JSON and lazy-class consumers. Keep independent semantic, TLS/socket and versioned-fact repairs moving. | Actual native/WASM semantic oracles, reentry and allocation-denial boundaries, final refcounts/charges, platform-specific execution, and measured latency/scaling/physical memory. Shared RuntimeHooks and ownership migrations have one integration owner. |
| 5 | Complete required installed LLVM delivery, compiler SDK behavior and package/extension closure under V1-02/V1-06/V1-24/V1-28. Existing LLVM coordinates and independent inventory wiring are already present. | Delivered feature/tool/runtime closure and actual installed public CLI execution without Cargo; current source-extension seals and complete native/WASM output companions. |
| 6 | Complete lossless output, actual execution identities and the positive measured-metric admission path early enough to support the final campaign. | One complete authenticated producer/receiver/installed-consumer path with independent omission/substitution controls, then the declared current-candidate semantic, performance/resource, reproducibility and supply-chain matrices, E1-E4 and H0. |

These dependencies allow parallel disjoint work; they do not require one giant
patch or universal new capabilities beyond the declared support contract. Every
existing open finding remains an exit obligation. Compiler startup/build/link
cost, emitted-guest execution/memory cost, development verification cost and
release supply-chain cost must be measured and attributed separately. Proof
capture and release signing stay on the development/release side; installed
users and emitted guests pay only for their required product behavior.

Machine-checked authorities remain `config/phase_exit_requirements.toml`,
`tools/phase_exit_manifest.py`, `tools/release_exit_gate.py`,
`tools/public_contract_gate.py` and the linked acceptance matrix above.
